154. **The virtual card carries the main traveler's name and steady last four digits (user, 2026-10-29: "1. Let's populate the name of the main profile in the card. 2. let's generate a random last 4 digits for the card").** Item 153's live card had an empty holder and number.
    - Name: `ProfileV3SettingsViewModel.mainTravellerName()` returns the hub's main traveller name (`profileName`, now stored, item 85) when it isn't blank, else the board store's cached `mainProfile.name`, else `loadLatestBoard()`, else nil. Settings passes it to `ProfileV3GloveBoxLiveFlow(mainTravellerName:)` and on to `ProfileV3GloveBoxViewModel(repository:isKYCComplete:mainTravellerName:)` (default `{ nil }`). `load()` and `createAddress()` ask for it after the first inbox page and keep `ProfileV3GloveBoxMapper.cardHolder(_:)` of it: the first word and the last word's initial ("Mohak Nahar" → "Mohak N.", "Venkata Satya Narayana Murthy" → "Venkata M.", "mohak n." stays), one word as is, blank or nil as "". The casing is the server's. The short form is the design's ("mohak n.") and fits the card's name column, which is one line about 130pt wide.
    - Digits: not random per open, which would change the number on every visit. `ProfileV3GloveBoxMapper.cardLastFour(for:)` is a 64-bit FNV-1a hash of the lower-cased glove box address, mod 10,000, zero-padded. The same user always gets the same four digits, on any device, with nothing stored; another address gets different ones. When the backend issues real cards, only `showInbox`'s two arguments change.
    - `ProfileV3GloveBoxMapper.home(email:cardHolder:cardLastFour:)` (both default "") feeds `card(holder:lastFour:)`, which replaces the static `card`. Paging, forwarding changes and email detail updates only rebuild the email section, so the card keeps its name and number.
    - Tests (`ProfileV3GloveBoxTests`, 25): `theCardShowsTheMainTravellersShortNameAndSteadyDigits` (the name rules; four digits, case-insensitive, different for another address) and `theLiveCardIsMadeOutToTheMainTraveller` (a live view model on `EmptyInboxStub` with "Priya Sharma" shows "Priya S." and the address's digits).
    - Verified: the glove box, Settings glove box, monogram and analytics suites, 49 tests, 0 errors and 0 warnings. Then the full suite on a throwaway iOS 26.5 simulator (deleted): 248 tests in 38 suites, 0 errors and 0 warnings, 245 pass, and the three failures are the known ones (item 123's deep-link test, item 139's two onboarding tests). A Release build has 0 errors and 0 warnings. Not run in Ayl, against Jaqen or on a device.
155. **Custom rules come from the agent screen and save with the agent (backend note pasted by the user, 2026-10-29: "Agent screens — custom rules. DEPLOYED 2026-10-29 (PR #23508, ai-memory + ai-kyc)").** Replaces item 136's on-device storage; item 140's editor layout and reveal stay.
    - Contract: flight, stay, airport_logistics, food and itinerary send a last field `{key: "custom_rules", label: "CUSTOM RULES", input: "custom_rules", value: [String] (oldest first, [] when none), add_label, editor {add_title, edit_title, note_label, save_label, saved_toast, max_chars: 500}, delete_confirm {title, confirm_label, cancel_label}, max_rules: 20, found?}`. Saving is the agent's normal `save_agent_preferences` with the complete snapshot, `custom_rules` being the whole list in order (`[]` after deleting the last), through the household sheet when `save.household` is served. A 422 `invalid_custom_rules` (over 500 characters, not text, or over 20 rules) saves nothing; the server trims and drops blanks. `found.total` counts the field.
    - Mapping: `ProfileV3AgentInput.customRules` and the field DTO's `add_label`, `max_rules`, `editor` and `delete_confirm` (both lossy) map to a section with the new `ProfileV3FormControl.customRules(ProfileV3CustomRulesField)` (rules trimmed with blanks dropped, labels "" when missing, `max_chars`/`max_rules` falling back to `Counts.customRuleMaxCharacters` 500 / `customRuleMaxRules` 20). `ProfileV3AgentSubmission` sends it as a list, so the rules ride in every agent save. The section's status follows the rules (complete with at least one). Every switch over form controls gained the case.
    - Model: `ProfileV3CustomRulesModel(title:field:commit:)` no longer has a repository. The form view model creates it from `form.customRulesSection` (`syncCustomRules`, from `form`'s `didSet`, so a refresh updates it; `customRules` is now `@Published`) while `isAgentCustomRulesEnabled`. Save and delete build the new list and call `commitCustomRules(_:)`, which writes it into the form section, then either opens the household sheet (`.handedOff`: the editor closes, and the household sheet does the save) or runs the save and returns `.saved`. The agent save is split into `beginSave()` and `performSave(profileIDs:)` so the editor can await it; `submit(profileIDs:)` and `confirmHousehold()` use the same steps, and the 1.2 s saved state now runs in its own `saveTask`. A failed save keeps the changed list in the form (unsaved, with the save bar) and the editor shows the error (`ProfileV3Error.message`, which is the server's text for a 422).
    - Display: cards are newest first (the server list reversed), keyed by server index; a reveal counter (`reveals`) replaces `revealedRuleID`. The editor's title, note label ("NOTE"), Save label and saved toast are the server's; the edit pill keeps "save changes" with the server's saved toast. `setDraft(_:)` caps the text at `maxCharacters`. The trash button now asks first: `ProfileV3CustomRuleDeleteSheet` (a fitted sheet over the editor) shows `ProfileV3ConfirmationView.deleteRule` with the server's title, a red confirm and the cancel label; confirm saves the shorter list and closes both, cancel returns to the editor.
    - "+ Custom" (`ProfileV3CustomRuleButton(title:accessibilityTitle:action:)`, the server's `add_label` and `add_title`) is always shown while the list is under `max_rules`, and hidden at the limit. When the save bar is up it sits on the left with "save changes" on the right (before, it gave way to the save bar). The form's section list skips the rules section (`ProfileV3FormSection.isCustomRules`); `ProfileV3CustomRulesSection` draws it after the other sections, with the server's label, only when there are rules.
    - Removed: `ProfileV3CustomRulesRepository`, `ProfileV3CustomRulesStore` and their Live and Mock conformances (rules saved on a device by item 136 are not migrated; that build was never tagged), and seven `ProfileV3Strings` (custom rule button, section title, add and edit titles, note label, save label, saved toast). Their `mo-aiprofile-agents-*` keys stay in the catalog, unused; they don't need adding to Ditto. `deleteRule` stays as the trash button's accessibility label.
    - Fixtures: `ProfileV3AgentFixtures.customRulesField(_:)` / `customRulesSection(_:)` carry the note's copy; the five rule agents' fixture forms end with the section (flight with two rules, food with one).
    - Tests: `ProfileV3CustomRulesTests` (9): the pasted field shape (rules trimmed, labels, editor, delete confirm, sent as `custom_rules` in the save body); adding saves the whole list (old plus new, no household IDs), shows the saved state, then closes and reveals; editing saves in place; deleting asks first, cancel sends nothing, confirm sends `[]`; a 422 shows the server's message; with a household the editor hands off and the household confirm sends the rules with both IDs; the draft cap and the `max_rules` limit; the two editor layout tests.
    - Render check (`ImageRenderer`, removed afterwards): the save bar with only "+ Custom", the dirty bar with "+ Custom" left and "save changes" right without overlap at 393pt, and the delete sheet (title, red Delete, Keep rule).
    - Verified: the custom rules, agent box, analytics, passport placeholder and previous passports suites, 57 tests, 0 errors and 0 warnings. Then the full suite on a throwaway iOS 26.5 simulator (deleted): 250 tests in 38 suites, 0 errors and 0 warnings, 247 pass, and the three failures are the known ones (item 123's deep-link test, item 139's two onboarding tests). A Release build has 0 errors and 0 warnings. The editor, the delete sheet and the household hand-off weren't seen running in a hosted window. Not run in Ayl, against the backend or on a device.

### GPU profiling: findings, fixes pending a decision (user, 2026-10-27: "do performance profiling. need to optimize the GPU usage"; screens: hub at rest with the carousel, and the expanded pass)

- Method: Metal System Trace (`--all-processes`) of Ayl Release on the iPhone 15 Plus (A16, 60 Hz). Ayl is launched with `devicectl … --payload-url ayl://profile`, which opens the hub with no taps. Temporary `PV3_GPU_CYCLE` builds switch the hub between modes every 10–12 s, and windows are aligned on the paused mode's GPU gap (the trace keeps no app logs). CPU is attributed with `time-profile` and the build's dSYM (`cpuclass.py`, `persecond_cpu.py`, `hubsyms.py`); the scripts and traces are in the scratch folder below.
- Checks before trusting a trace:
  - The binary is `Ayl`, not `Ayl.debug.dylib`. An Xcode run replaced the Release build at 15:51.
  - AI-Native now takes AI-Profile from GitHub: `Ayl.xcodeproj/project.xcproj` points at `https://github.com/atlyslabs/AI-Profile`, and `Package.resolved` pins `e94a37e`, uncommitted there. Local edits reach Ayl only if they are patched into `/tmp/ayl-device-dd/SourcePackages/checkouts/AI-Profile` and built with `-disableAutomaticPackageResolution`. Confirm with `nm` on the dSYM; `strings` misses Swift strings of 15 bytes or fewer.
  - The disk has room. A save at 99% full lost the data without an error.
- Baseline (13:35, glass on): Ayl ~110 ms/s (`RBLayer:root-layer`, 23 frames/s on the 24 fps ambient clock, about 4.5 ms each). backboardd ~120 ms/s steady, plus ~340 ms every 5 s when `ProfileV3BenefitCardStack` rotates. Together that is about 20–25% of the GPU at rest.
- `e94a37e` (glass off, card shadow on the shape, stack paused under covers), 16:58: Ayl ~105, backboardd ~95–100 steady, spike ~230–320. Glass off trims backboardd's backdrop passes; Ayl is unchanged.
- Attribution, in 10 s windows on the current code (Ayl ms/s / backboardd steady ms/s):

  | Mode | Ayl | backboardd |
  |---|---|---|
  | paused | 0 | 3–5 |
  | current | 116–119 | 86–121 |
  | no card globe | 0 | 82–126 |
  | no card hover | 109–120 | 10–43 |
  | no globe and no hover | 0 | 4–5 |
  | globe `Canvas(rendersAsynchronously: true)` | 98 | 139 |
  | globe in `.drawingGroup()` | 125 | 91 |
  | globe drawn by `ImageRenderer` into an `Image` or a `UIImageView` | 96–121 (`RBImageRenderer`) | 86–91 |
  | hover without `ProfileV3HoverShadow` | 95 | 77 |
  | card shadow new / old / none | 118 / 94 / 111 | 121 / 143 / 115 (spike 443 / 358 / 212) |
  | PostHog replay stopped | 154 | 139 |

  - **The card globe is all of Ayl's GPU at rest.** Drawing it costs about 4.5 ms of GPU a frame wherever it goes: in the root layer, in its own layer, or through `ImageRenderer`. The ~2,000 land dots plus the `landTint` path of ~2,000 overlapping discs (`cardAppearance.landTint`; the expanded appearance has none) go through RenderBox 24 times a second.
  - **The card hover is most of backboardd's steady work** (~75 ms/s). That is the moving card's offscreen passes (`Read Surface … → Write Surface`) and fragment work. The hover shadow overlay is ~9 ms/s of it. The card is clipped by a continuous rounded rectangle (`ProfileV3WorldPassCard.swift:57`).
  - The earlier experiment's "glass off gives Ayl 0" (13:49 build) doesn't reproduce: today glass off still renders the globe every frame. Its window assignment checked out, so the cause of that zero is unknown.
  - The card shadow change gives no measurable gain, and its one spike sample is worse than the old shadow's.
- Options, undecided:
  - Globe: drop the card `landTint`, which item 92 measured at ~1.4 of 2.17 ms CPU per frame; this needs a run to measure the GPU side and is a visual change. Spin the card globe at 12 fps (about half). Draw the land as point sprites in Metal (the stopped work in `stash@{0}`; ~0.1 ms a frame). Or keep the card globe still and spin it only when expanded.
  - Hover: find and remove the moving card's offscreen effect, lower its frame rate, or stop it.
  - Card shadow: revert to the shadow on the whole card (in `e94a37e`).
- PostHog session replay (Ayl config, not the package): `AI-Native/Ayl/Core/Analytics/PostHogAnalytics.swift:24-25` turns on `sessionReplay` with `screenshotMode`, with the default `throttleDelay` of 1 s and no `sampleRate`. On the hub it costs ~470–570 ms/s of Ayl CPU: the vendored libwebp (`PHVP8…`, `*_NEON`) on background queues, plus `PostHogGraphicsImageRenderer.image(actions:)` on the main thread. That is more than 80% of Ayl's CPU, and it continues while the hub is paused and static. The profile package itself is ~11–20 ms/s of main-thread CPU, almost all `drawLand`. It doesn't change GPU time.
- Not measured: the expanded pass. Every run was the hub at rest.

Still open:

1. **CI and distribution.** Resolved for CI by vdcios `50ce1e9e1`: the app references `https://github.com/atlyslabs/AI-Profile` on branch `master`. Still open: a branch reference moves with every push, so switch to a tagged version before release.
2. **Xcode on CI.** The CI workflows run on self-hosted Mac minis and don't pin Xcode. If any of them is on Xcode 26, lower `swift-tools-version` to 6.2. `ApproachableConcurrency` needs 6.2, and the manifest's trailing commas need 6.1.
3. **Tests.** `VDCAppTests/ProfileV3MapperTests.swift` is excluded from the `feature-profile-ai-spm` test target so it does not compile against the removed module. 4. **Delete the old module.** `Modules/ProfileV3` is unlinked and still on disk. The VDCApp build succeeded without it. Delete the folder, then freeze that copy.
5. **Sync the app's host copy (done in vdcios on 2026-10-15, uncommitted on `feature-profile-ai-spm`).** `Modules/ProfileV3Host` now matches this repo's working-tree `AppIntegration/ProfileV3Host`, including the runtime rework (Done item 41). `VDCProfileV3BaseProvider` conforms to `ProfileV3ConfigurationProviding` and carries `currentUserID` and `isGuestSession`. `VDCProfileV3Composition` registers with `ProfileV3Runtime`. `VDCProfileV3Session` and `VDCProfileV3NavigationHost` are deleted. `ProfileTabBarManager` calls `makeHubSurface(onClose:)`. ATCaptureKit runs the models itself through the app's registered runner. That file is copied back here too. The runtime rework is pushed (`6bd8f60`). Later on 2026-10-15 the user removed the Xcode folder references to `../AI-Profile` and `../IdentityCapture`, so the app resolves both packages from GitHub `master` (`Package.resolved` pins `6bd8f60` here and ATCaptureKit `2b90a24`).
6. **Follow the rename in the app (done in vdcios on 2026-10-15, uncommitted).** Product dependency `CA9CCA2A307F9B36006E93C3` has `productName = ATAIProfile`, and the 16 imports read `import ATAIProfile`. A full VDCApp Debug simulator build in its own `-derivedDataPath` succeeds against the local package.

### Decisions pending

1. **Distribution:** decided in vdcios `50ce1e9e1`: the remote atlyslabs repo, branch `master`. Tags exist since 2026-10-19: `1.0.0` and `1.0.2` to `1.0.11` on GitHub, and `1.0.1` local only (see Upstream drift). The README tells hosts to use `from:`. `CHANGELOG.md` on `origin/master` has a `1.0.9` entry but none for `1.0.2`–`1.0.8`, `1.0.10` or `1.0.11`; the last two are under Unreleased. Pending: switching the app's package reference from `branch: master` to a version, and bringing the README version line (still 1.0.1) up to the latest tag.
2. **Identity Capture inside ATAIProfile:** the app now links `ATCaptureKit` (local `../IdentityCapture`, 2026-10-15) and `VDCProfileV3IdentityCaptureHost` builds the flow from it with the app's scan engines. Still open here: depend on the `ATCaptureKit` package (folder `IdentityCapture`) directly for `IDCFactory`, `IDCFlowView`, and `IDCConfiguration`. Do not keep `ProfileV3IdentityCaptureHost` once those types are in the package. `Application/` and the OCR verifiers are in `IdentityCapture/AppIntegration` and are not compiled. The debug screen, the on-device checkers, the KYC passport guide, analytics, and `AVNavigationViewController` still need to leave the package sources.
3. **Settings status messages:** `FlashMessageGenerator` returns early unless DEBUG, so in Release only the haptic plays. Either keep that through the host, or show the text in Release with `ProfileV3Toast`, which first needs a failure style.

### Known trade-offs

- `ATCoreServerError` carries only a message and a status, so since Done item 61 the envelope plugin parks the parsed envelope (`code`, ids) in one shared slot, `ProfileV3ServerFailureBox.shared`, for `ProfileV3APIErrorMapper` to consume. That slot holds only the latest failure. Two requests failing at once can swap envelopes, and a later error with no response (network, decoding) can pick up a stale envelope, because successes don't clear it. Carrying the envelope inside the error would need ATCore to keep the response body.
- `ATCoreCacheConfig` defaults to an expiration of `7 * 24 * 60` seconds, about 2.8 hours, which looks like a units slip. The package passes an explicit seven-day expiry. Worth raising with the ATCore owners.
- On the first launch after switchover the board comes from the network, because its cache moves from `DataManager` to ATCore's persistent cache. The old `DataManager` entries are orphaned.

## Gotchas

- The app's ProfileV3 is still changing, so diff before editing. See Building.
- Errors hide errors. See the stub build in Building.
- The main app target builds in Swift 5.0 without approachable concurrency. The `SWIFT_APPROACHABLE_CONCURRENCY` lines in the pbxproj belong to a different target.
- `ATCoreCacheService.shared` sweeps entries older than 15 minutes, and it hashes plain `String` keys with a per-process `Hasher`. Use `.persistent` with `ATCoreDeterministicCacheKeyProvider` keys.
- ATCore only reads flat `message` or `error` strings from error bodies.
- `KYCOnboardingCoordinator` starts its flow from a task that holds the coordinator weakly, so the caller must keep it alive until the flow ends.
- The settings presenter defers `onFinish` while KYC onboarding runs, and reports it exactly once.
- Four files use ATUIKit's `Color(at:)` without `import ATUIKit`: `ProfileV3PassportScanLauncher`, `Components/ProfileV3DurationWheel`, `Support/ProfileV3Theme+SettingsPage` and `Support/ProfileV3Theme+PastTripsSizes`. They compile only because extension members leak across files, and would break under `MemberImportVisibility`.
- macOS's periodic `/tmp` cleanup empties `/tmp/aiprofile-dd/SourcePackages/checkouts` after a few days unused. It leaves the folders and `.git` stubs but no `Package.swift`, so resolution fails with "the package manifest … cannot be accessed". Delete `/tmp/aiprofile-dd/SourcePackages` and let `xcodebuild` clone again.
- In Claude Code, parallel shell commands share one working directory, so use absolute paths. zsh doesn't split unquoted variables into words, so put flag arrays in bash scripts.
- `#expect(values.allSatisfy(\.flag))` doesn't compile: swift-testing's macro calls `allSatisfy` (rethrows) through a closure that can throw ("call can throw, but it is not marked with 'try'"). Put the result in a local first. That error line starts with `macro expansion #expect`, so the Building section's `grep '^/.*error:'` misses it; count `error:` anywhere, or check for `** TEST FAILED **` with no test results.

## Verifying the app-side code

The app-side files can't build inside the package. They were typechecked with a two-stage harness before the local package was linked. The harness needs a refresh against the real package once the VDCApp build has run.

1. Build a stand-in `ATAIProfile` module in Swift 6 mode with `ApproachableConcurrency`. Use the `Host/*.swift` files except `ProfileV3Environment.swift`, the public types from `Domain/ProfileV3KYCCapture.swift` plus `ProfileV3PassportSide`, and a stub with the real public signatures of `ProfileV3Environment` and `ProfileV3EntryPoint.configure`.
2. Typecheck `AppIntegration/ProfileV3Host/*.swift` against it in Swift 5 mode. Include these real app sources: KYC `Domain/Models/` KYCConnectorStep, KYCPassportStep, KYCPassportUpload and KYCMinimal; `KYCCaptureCopy` extracted from `KYCStep.swift`; the particle-globe section of `KYCValueCardView.swift`; IdentityCapture `Domain/` IDCConfiguration, IDCCallbacks, IDCPiece and IDCVerifier. Add stubs copied from the real signatures for the KYC views, coordinator, factory, OAuth and analytics, the IDC factory, view model and view, `IDCStrings`, `IDCTheme`, AppDelegate, `APIConstants`, `HomeIdentityProvider`, `AnalyticsEventManager`, `UserData` and `GuestSession`.

Both stages passed with no errors or warnings. The harness needs `-I` and `-F` pointing at the package build products, and `-Xcc -fmodule-map-file=` for every module map in `GeneratedModuleMaps-iphonesimulator` except `ATAIProfile.modulemap`. It also needs the CocoaLumberjack include directories. The original script was `harness-build.sh` in a temporary session scratchpad and may be gone.
